The 07-27 row is left in place with its own date and is not edited.** ⚠️ **NOTHING ELSE SUPERSEDED — no WMB rating, price target, estimate, EBITDA/CAGR figure, GW target or leverage mark on this page was touched. The note carries NO WMB PT, rating or estimate**; WMB's bucket placement is unchanged (*"Behind-the-Meter (BTM) project developers… US Overweights: SEI, VST, LBRT, INIO, WMB, and EROC"*), and the earlier MS marks (OW ~25% upside screen 2026-04-06; *"the broadest integrated model"* 07-20 / 07-27; the on-site power slot in all three States of the World 08-12) all stand. 🆕 **NET-NEW AND MATERIAL:** MS's first full WMB stock paragraph from this franchise, which makes an explicit **above-guidance AND above-consensus** growth-capex/EBITDA call catalysed by *"the next set of power projects"* — the same side of the argument JSD took on 05-06 — plus a **18-24 months from contract execution** cycle-time mark for WMB's onsite generation and a *"multi-year head start on behind-the-meter power solutions"*. Also new: MS expects *"a large number of Behind-the-Meter (BTM) power generation deals announced in the coming months, with an especially large concentration in **West Texas**"*, and names **Louisiana, West Virginia, Texas and several Upper Midwest states** as likely to surprise to the upside. ⚠️ **TWO GUARDS.** ⓵ **MS's own caveat on its revision: *"part of the Net Shortfall increase was the elimination of a double-counting of PSP sites, artificially reducing the shortfall by ~15GW in the previous model"*** — the gross-demand raise (68 → 97 GW) is the NVL72 rack re-spec, but most of the NET widening is bookkeeping, and a sales relay of this note circulating the same day attributes the whole move to the rack re-spec. ⓶ **BASIS: the note's PSP economics ($10-12/watt build, 15-19% unlevered FCF yields, $15,500m of net value creation per GW, $4.5/watt for a one-year time advantage) are BUILD-cost and value-creation figures, not revenue per watt — do not chain them into any $/GW or $/W series on this wiki.** ⚠️ **The 18-24 month figure is MS's class-level claim for WMB's offering, not a delivered average across its own projects; treat it as a bull's number pending the Socrates phase-two proof point at YE26.** |

MS's stated mechanism: *"memory become a key bottleneck due to rack complexity."* Plus the MU paragraph — *"direct exposure to the memory bottleneck across DRAM, high-bandwidth memory, and NAND… we expect shortages to **intensify into 2027-28**."* ⚠️⚠️⚠️ **THE GUARD THAT GOVERNS EVERY ONE OF THOSE FIGURES AND IS WRITTEN INTO EVERY PLACE THEY APPEAR: THIS IS A COST SERIES, NOT A BIT SERIES. The +435% is the product of accelerators per rack (MS describes the transition as *"72 GPUs on a rack versus extant 8-per-server"* and elsewhere as *"NVL72 or 144"*), GB per accelerator, and $/GB — and MS decomposes none of the three. Against this page's existing marks (HBM4 $30-32/GB vs $17-18/GB HBM3-class, Fubon 08-03; Mizuho's 288GB flat to VR200 then DOWN to 256GB HBM4e at VR-Ultra, 08-16) price and count can carry the whole move with GB-per-GPU flat or falling. It therefore does NOT refute the de-spec / 4-hi bear, the `HBM content per GPU` Signal row is UNCHANGED at ⚠ nuance, and the number must not be converted into bits.** ⚠️ **EXTRACTION DEFECT RECORDED SO IT IS NOT RE-LITIGATED: MS's Exhibit 51 ("Generation Comparison") has COLUMN HEADERS SHIFTED ONE POSITION in the PDF extraction — its "Blackwell" column carries Exhibit 30's VR200 memory figure ($2,001,600) and its rack-power row (14.3 / 234 / 600 / 1,100 / 1,540 kW) contradicts the note's own prose (*"234 kW for Vera Rubin… 600 kW from 415 kW for Rubin Ultra"*). Exhibit 51 is NOT quoted anywhere on this page and none of its numbers — including its 11% / 46% / 53% / 58% / 60% memory-as-%-of-GPU-cost ladder — are adopted. Exhibits 30 and 33 are row-labelled and tie, and are the only ones used.** 📎 **One item that reads negative and is not: MS cut *"storage as a % of IT power to 5% from 10%, indicative of up-to-date disclosures by NVIDIA and IEA"* — a POWER-BUDGET assumption (storage draws fewer watts), NOT a spend or capacity assumption. The 09-21 primary re-marks it to **11 / 19 / 49 GW (low / midpoint / high) at a 90% probability of success** — the midpoint moves only 18 → 19 GW but **the high end more than doubles from 20 GW to 49 GW**, and it remains comfortably the largest of the four remedies (Bloom fuel cells 5 / 6 / 8 @90%, DC at an operational nuclear plant 1 / 3 / 8 @75%, PSP-site conversion 8 / 13 / 19 @90% but now EXCLUDED from the reduction as already counted in DCs under construction or in the turbine line).** 🆕 **NET-NEW (read-through):** the rebuilt envelope — **97 GW of 2026-28 US DC power demand from 68 GW, less 21 GW under construction and 19 GW grid access = 57 GW gross from 38 GW**, probability-weighted remedies **15 / 24 / 57 GW** → net **(42) / (33) / (0) GW = 43% / 34% / 0%**, and **165 GW / 107 GW gross / 72-82 GW net through 2029**; MS's expectation of *"a large number of Behind-the-Meter (BTM) power generation deals announced in the coming months, with an especially large concentration in **West Texas**"*; and — with GEV unmentioned in the passage — a **fourth house on this page's live SpaceX blades-and-vanes debate**, which lands with the skeptics: **3-4 GW of accessible gas-turbine power for SpaceX by YE27**, *"total reported commitments closer to **8 GW**"*, terrestrial compute *"peaking at **~15 GW in 2031**"*, hot-section castings *"a market **controlled by a handful of casters already backlogged for years**"*, and the Bastrop foundry ramp *"may take **MULTIPLE YEARS** given the process complexity, equipment lead times, and workforce build-out involved in single crystal casting"* — plus a framing new to this page, that the foundry could open *"the door to **partnerships with existing turbine manufacturers**"* rather than to competition. ⚠️ **MS does NOT resolve the aeroderivative-vs-IGT question (Bernstein says IGT, 22V says aeroderivative); the passage says only "datacenter turbines and Raptor turbopumps." The MS "Power Struggle" (07-14) shortfall architecture that this page has carried as its sharpest bear argument since 08-20 — *"38 GW power bottleneck over 2026-28, down from our prior 47 GW for 2025-28"*; the waterfall **68 − 15 under construction − 15 grid access = 38**; remedies **nat-gas turbines 15/18/20 @90% · BLOOM 5/7/8 @90% · nuclear co-location −/3/5 @75% · bitcoin conversion 10/14/19 @90%** → **27/37/46 GW**, net **(11)/(1)/none = 16%/2%/0%**, with MS labelling the midpoint *"the most likely outcome"* — IS SUPERSEDED BY THE 09-21 PRIMARY:** **97 GW of 2026-28 US DC power demand − 21 GW of DCs under construction − 19 GW of utility grid access = 57 GW GROSS (*"a 50% increase from our previous estimate"*)**; remedies **BTM turbines and reciprocating engines 11 / 19 / 49 @90% · BLOOM ENERGY FUEL CELLS 5 / 6 / 8 @90% · site a DC at an operational nuclear plant 1 / 3 / 8 @75% · convert PSP sites 8 / 13 / 19 @90% BUT NOW EXPLICITLY EXCLUDED from the reduction** → probability-weighted **15 / 24 / 57 GW** → **NET (42) / (33) / (0) GW = 43% / 34% / 0%**, midpoint again *"most likely"*; extended to 2029, **165 GW of demand → 107 GW gross / 72-82 GW net = 44-49%**, annual cumulative net **5 / 12 / 33 / 72 GW** with the **Bloom line at 1 / 2 / 5 / 9 GW**. 🔴 **THE SIGN FLIPS AND THE BEAR MUTATES. The "gap is closing to ~1 GW by 2028" argument is retired by the house that created it. What replaces it is a RELATIVE-SHARE bear: Bloom's midpoint is CUT to 6 GW from 7 GW while BTM turbines and engines are raised to a 19 GW midpoint and a 49 GW high (from 20 GW), so on the high case MS models 49 GW of engines against 8 GW of fuel cells — a 6:1 ratio versus a prior 2.5:1. Nuclear co-siting, which 07-14 had cut to 0-5 GW in BE's favour, recovers to 1 / 3 / 8 GW at 75%.** ✅ **ARITHMETIC VERIFIED IN-HOUSE BEFORE ADOPTION, so the two exhibits can be used together: 19×0.9 + 6×0.9 + 3×0.75 = 24.75 ≈ MS's printed 24 GW midpoint; 11×0.9 + 5×0.9 + 1×0.75 = 15.15 ≈ 15; 49×0.9 + 8×0.9 + 8×0.75 = 57.3 ≈ 57; and 57 − 24 = 33. The apparent 6-vs-5 discrepancy between the Bloom bucket and the Bloom annual line is just the 90% weighting — not an inconsistency.** ⚠️⚠️ **GUARD CARRIED INTO THE BODY, AND IT IS THE THING THE 21h RELAY OMITS: MS discounts its own headline — *"part of the Net Shortfall increase was the elimination of a DOUBLE-COUNTING of PSP sites, artificially reducing the shortfall by ~15GW in the previous model."* Roughly half the 32 GW net move is an accounting correction to MS's own prior model. The genuine raise is on GROSS DEMAND, 68 → 97 GW, and that is the 8-GPU-server → NVL72 rack re-spec. Size the thesis off the demand raise, not off the net shortfall.** 🔴 **TWO FURTHER CORRECTIONS TO THE 21h RELAY ENTRY, RECORDED HERE AND NOT DELETED FROM IT: the note is titled *"Powering AI: Racking Up the Power Shortfall"*, not *"Power Shortfall Update"*; and the cover lead authors are **Stephen C Byrd and James E Faucette**, with Arcaro one of eight further contributors.** 🆕 **NET-NEW:** BE's own MS stock paragraph (*"one of the most direct solutions to the time-to-power bottleneck… secure onsite power BEFORE grid interconnections arrive… INTERCONNECTION TIMELINES EXTEND BEYOND FIVE YEARS"*), which is the permitting/speed moat this page already treats as its best-corroborated leg, now stated by MS in the same terms; BE named as one of **three US Overweights in power-generation equipment alongside GEV and CMI**; and the annual path of the shortfall, which this page has never had. ⚠️ **NOTHING ELSE SUPERSEDED — no BE rating, price target, estimate, guide, MW figure or valuation mark was replaced. The shape is TWO STEP-CHANGES, not a smooth exponential (Apr→May +9pp = 4x in one month; Jun→Jul +8pp; +2pp plateaus between). ⚠️ The model-release explanation for the steps is MY inference — the document names no model version against any month.** ⚠️⚠️ **THE GOVERNING CAVEAT, FROM ANTHROPIC'S OWN METHODOLOGY AND RECORDED PROMINENTLY: *"model-versus-human exact agreement was 59%, human-versus-human was 35%"*, within one level 97%. Humans agree with each other on the exact automation level only about a third of the time, and the AL3/AL4 boundary is exactly where the 26% is cut. The page treats the LEVEL as soft and the SLOPE as the signal.** 🆕 **ALSO NET-NEW AND FIRST-PARTY: ~30,000 agents doing R&E work at Anthropic AT ANY ONE TIME on its most-used internal platform; over a BILLION monitored agent decisions in August 2026 with 0.002% (≈1 in 47,000) blocked by a real-time monitor covering 100% of actions; offline classifiers flagging ~100,000 transcripts/week with ~50/week escalated to humans; and the first safety-compute split published by any lab — ~6% of AI R&D compute and ~12% of AI-DRIVEN AI R&D compute, over the single week 13-20 July 2026.** 🔴 **DERIVED FROM THE DISCLOSED FIGURES ONLY (no external chaining), ALL FLOORS AT ONE SIGNIFICANT FIGURE because *"over a billion"* is a floor and *"approximately 30,000"* is 1sf: ~33,000 monitored decisions per CONCURRENT AGENT-SLOT in August, ~1,000/slot/day, ~370 decisions/sec aggregate, one decision per slot every ~80s or faster; blocked actions ~15,000-25,000 in August (0.002% at 1sf spans 0.0015-0.0025%) ≈ ~3,400-5,600/week into human review.** ⚠️ **FOUR BASIS WARNINGS CARRIED ON THE PAGE: (1) *"~30,000 at any one time"* is a CONCURRENCY STOCK, so this is per agent-SLOT and not per agent — more distinct agents than 30,000 may have run during the month; (2) scope is *"this platform only"*, not Anthropic's agent population; (3) the source says *"decisions"* but defines coverage over *"actions"* and never equates them; (4) a duty-cycle variant was WITHDRAWN — the population is already defined as agents *"doing research and engineering work… at any one time"*, so an idleness discount double-counts.** ⛔ **AND ONE CLAIM WITHDRAWN IN DRAFT AND RECORDED AS SUCH, BECAUSE IT WAS THE SAME FALLACY THIS ENTRY REJECTS BELOW: an earlier version cited the source's *"0.002%"* and *"about 1 in 47,000"* as two statements agreeing within 6%, presented as a source-quality check. They are ONE measurement printed at two precisions — 1/47,000 = 0.0021277%, which rounds to 0.002% — carrying zero corroborative information. Caught by the pre-publication skeptic, not after the fact.** ⛔ **DELIBERATELY NOT DERIVED, WITH THE REASONING RECORDED SO IT IS NOT RE-DERIVED LATER: an internal token-burn figure. Chaining the wiki's un-adopted ~41 tok/s/agent (from the 09-17 Dwarkesh/Noam Brown 130bn-token assertion) onto 30,000 agents gives a plausible-looking ~3.3 trillion tokens for August at ~3,294 tokens/decision — but running it the other way (Anthropic's decision rate applied to the OpenAI run) returns 3,297, THE SAME NUMBER, because it is one equation rearranged, not an independent check. One equation, two unknowns; neither identified. The bases also differ (burst-at-full-tilt vs standing population with unknown idle). No token-burn number entered.** ⚠️ **ALSO FLAGGED AND NOT RESOLVED: the offline monitor is described BOTH as flagging ~100,000 transcripts/week AND as flagging "one to two transcripts in every thousand" — the two cannot be reconciled from the document, so NO total-transcript figure was derived. And the person-time weighting (*"Each person gets one unit of weight per week, split evenly across the tasks they worked on"*) is a task-COUNT weighting, not an hours weighting — a five-minute task and a four-day task carry equal weight, which would bias the index high if AI-led tasks are the small numerous ones. MY observation, not Anthropic's.** 🔴 **CREDIT WHERE DUE: Anthropic pre-empted the obvious frozen-basket attack by rebuilding the tree from January 2026 data and comparing new tasks Feb→Jul; it *"found no rise in the number of 'novel' tasks"*, and each month is rated only on evidence from that month or earlier, so this is a genuine time series rather than a back-cast.** |
